\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}bb\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}

\displaystyle\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}bb\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}

\displaystyle\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}.

We claim that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
. Note that there is no,

y_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}
be such that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}y_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}

and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}y_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}
are
well-formed
.
So, let
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}
be such that
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}

and
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
are
well-formed
:

\sigma
must be of type
\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}
, but then
\mathcal{R}
is invariant under removing prefixes of type
\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}
, and so
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\mathcal{R}
and

x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\mathcal{R}
. And hence,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
. Similarly,

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\quad\left(\begin{smallmatrix}b\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\begin{smallmatrix}b\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}.\quad

The dual argument (using now the fact that both sides do
not
belong to
\mathcal{R}
) shows that

\varepsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\varepsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\quad\varepsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
bb\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\quad\left(\begin{smallmatrix}bb\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\varepsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\begin{smallmatrix}bb\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\mathcal{R}}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
bb\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}.

The case of types
\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}
and
\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}
follows by symmetry.

Appendix E
Details on Pseudovarieties

E.1
Proof of the Lifting Theorem

\liftingtheoremmonoids

*

Proof.

(1)
\Rightarrow
(2).
Since
\mathcal{R}
is a
\mathcal{V}
-relation
, there exists

\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{V}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\Sigma^{\smash{2}}_{\smash{\hskip 0.22083pt\raisebox{1.22496pt}{\scriptsize\hskip 0.39864pt\rule{2.3917pt}{0.59789pt}\hskip 0.39864pt
}}}}\!}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}=\mathcal{L}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\textsf{WellFormed}_{\Sigma}}
.
Hence, there exists a morphism of monoids
\varphi\colon({\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\Sigma^{\smash{2}}_{\smash{\hskip 0.24582pt\raisebox{1.43494pt}{\scriptsize\hskip 0.39864pt\rule{2.3917pt}{0.59789pt}\hskip 0.39864pt
}}}}\!)^{*}\to M
such
that
M\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathcal{L}=\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
for some
\textrm{Acc}\subseteq M
.
It follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}=\mathcal{L}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\textsf{WellFormed}_{\Sigma}}
rewrites as
“for all
u\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\textsf{WellFormed}_{\Sigma}}
,
\varphi(u)\in\textrm{Acc}

iff

u\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}
”.
Letting
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{M}}
be the
synchronous algebra induced by the monoid

M
, define

\psi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{M}}
by
\psi(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})\mathrel{\hat{=}}(\varphi(u))_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}
for
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}
.
Let
\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\mid x\in\textrm{Acc}\land\sigma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathcal{T}}\!\}
.
We claim that
\psi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}]={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}
. Indeed, for
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}
,

u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}

iff

u\in\mathcal{L}
,

i.e.

\varphi(u)\in\textrm{Acc}
,
that is
\psi(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})=(\varphi(u))_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}
.
Notice then that all
underlying monoids
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{M}}
are
M
,
and hence they belong to
\mathbb{V}
.

(2)
\Rightarrow
(3).
By
Lemma

18
,
the
syntactic synchronous algebra
of
\mathcal{R}

divides

any
algebra

\mathbf{B}

recognizing

\mathcal{R}
.
In particular, its
underlying monoids

divide
the
underlying monoids
of
\mathbf{B}
. The conclusion follows since
\mathbb{V}
is closed under
division
.

(3)
\Rightarrow
(1).
Denote by
M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt},M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
and
M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
the

underlying monoids
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
. Let
\textrm{Acc}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
be the accepting set
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}^{-1}\!}[\textrm{Acc}]
.
Define
M\mathrel{\hat{=}}M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}\times M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}\times M_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
, and

\varphi\colon

({\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\Sigma^{\smash{2}}_{\smash{\hskip 0.24582pt\raisebox{1.43494pt}{\scriptsize\hskip 0.39864pt\rule{2.3917pt}{0.59789pt}\hskip 0.39864pt
}}}}\!)^{*}

\to

M

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\mapsto

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\mapsto

\langle 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\mapsto

\langle 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle

and finally, let

\displaystyle\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\cup\bigl\{\langle 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,z_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle\mid z_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\cup\bigl\{\langle x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,y_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle\mid x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\cdot y_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\cup\bigl\{\langle x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},\,z_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\rangle\mid x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\cdot z_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}\bigr\}.

We first claim that

For every
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}
,

\varphi(u)
is of the form
\langle a,\,b,\,1\rangle

and moreover,

()

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=a\cdot b
,

which can trivially be proven by induction on
u
. Analogous results
hold for words of different
type
.
We then prove that for each
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})\in\textrm{Acc}\quad\text{{iff}}\quad\varphi(u)\in\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}.\immediate\immediate

()

The direct implication is straightforward, using

Equation

()
.
The converse implication is more tricky: assume
e.g.
that
\sigma={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 1.00006pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 1.00006pt
,
say
t_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}=u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}v_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
.
If
\varphi(t)\in\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}
, using
Equation

()
then it implies either that

1.

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(v_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
, and
1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}
, or

2.

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(v_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\in\textrm{Acc}
, or even

3.

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(v_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\cdot 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}
.

Clearly,
(2)
implies the desired conclusion, namely that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(t_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}){\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(v_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\in\textrm{Acc}
.
In all other cases, we will make heavy use of the
dependency relation
.
For case
(1)
, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(t_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})=1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
.
From
1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}
,
Fact

12
yields
1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\cdot 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}=1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}
, since
Acc
is
closed
.
Lastly, in case
(3)
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\asymp}}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\cdot 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in\textrm{Acc}
so

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\in\textrm{Acc}
and hence

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(t_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\cdot 1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\asymp}}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\in\textrm{Acc}
which yields

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}(t_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})\in\textrm{Acc}
. This concludes the proof
of (
()
‣
E.1
) for
type

\sigma={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 1.00006pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 1.00006pt
.
Other
types
are handled similarly, and hence

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}=\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}^{\prime}]\cap{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\textsf{WellFormed}_{\Sigma}}
.
∎

E.2
Proof of Lemma
26

Proposition 33
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathbf{A}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{B}
be a surjective
morphism
,
and
Acc
be a
closed subset
of
\mathbf{B}
. Let
a,a^{\prime}\in\mathbf{A}
.
Then

a\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]}}}a^{\prime}\quad\text{{iff}}\quad\varphi(a)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.02228pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.37993pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.81778pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\textrm{Acc}}}}\varphi(a^{\prime}).

Proof.

Direct implication.

Pick any
b_{\ell},b_{r}\in\mathbf{B}
such that both

b_{\ell}\varphi(a)b_{r}
and

b_{\ell}\varphi(a^{\prime})b_{r}

are well-defined. By surjectivity of
\varphi
, there exists

a_{\ell},a_{r}\in\mathbf{A}

such that
\varphi(a_{\ell})=b_{\ell}

and
\varphi(a_{r})=b_{r}
.
Then both
a_{\ell}aa_{r}

and
a_{\ell}a^{\prime}a_{r}
are well-defined since they have the same
type

as
b_{\ell}\varphi(a)b_{r}
and
b_{\ell}\varphi(a^{\prime})b_{r}
, respectively.
From
a\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]}}}a^{\prime}
,
it follows that
a_{\ell}aa_{r}
belongs to
\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]

iff

a_{\ell}a^{\prime}a_{r}
does.
And hence

b_{\ell}\varphi(a)b_{r}\in\textrm{Acc}\quad\text{{iff}}\quad b_{\ell}\varphi(a^{\prime})b_{r}\in\textrm{Acc}.

Converse implication.

Dually, pick any
a_{\ell},a_{r}\in\mathbf{A}
such that both

a_{\ell}aa_{r}
and
a_{\ell}a^{\prime}a_{r}
are well-defined.
Then
\varphi(a_{\ell})\varphi(a)\varphi(a_{r})
and
\varphi(a_{\ell})\varphi(a^{\prime})\varphi(a_{r})

are also well-defined since they have the same
type
as their preimage,
and
\varphi(a)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\textrm{Acc}}}}\varphi(a^{\prime})
implies that the element
\varphi(a_{\ell})\varphi(a)\varphi(a_{r})
belongs
to
Acc

iff

\varphi(a_{\ell})\varphi(a^{\prime})\varphi(a_{r})
does. It follows
that
a_{\ell}aa_{r}\in\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]

iff

a_{\ell}a^{\prime}a_{r}\in\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
.
∎

Proposition 34

(Inverse images of surjective
morphisms
preserve
residuals
)
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathbf{A}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{B}
be a surjective
morphism
, and
\textrm{Acc}\subseteq\mathbf{B}

be a
closed subset
. Let
u\in\mathbf{A}
. Then

u^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]=\varphi^{-1}[\varphi(u)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\textrm{Acc}].

Proof.

Left-to-right inclusion.

Let
a\in u^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
.
Then there exists
a^{\prime}\in\mathbf{A}
such that
a\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]}}}a^{\prime}

and
ua^{\prime}\in\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
.
By
Proposition

33

a\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]}}}a^{\prime}
implies
\varphi(a)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\textrm{Acc}}}}\varphi(a^{\prime})
,
and
ua^{\prime}\in\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
yields
\varphi(u)\varphi(a^{\prime})\in\textrm{Acc}
.
Overall, this shows that
a\in\varphi^{-1}[\varphi(u)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\textrm{Acc}]
.

Right-to-left inclusion.

Let
a\in\varphi^{-1}[\varphi(u)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\textrm{Acc}]
. Then

\varphi(a)\in\varphi(u)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}-1}}\textrm{Acc}

\varphi(a)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.00006pt}{\scalebox{.6}{$\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\frown$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{3.5764pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\raisebox{1.34995pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.80002pt}}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{$\smile$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip 5.0pt}}$}}}_{\textrm{Acc}}}}b^{\prime}

. By surjectivity of

, there exists

such that

and

.

E.3
Proof of Theorem
27

See
27

Proof.

We very roughly follow the proof schema of
[
Pin22
, XIII.4, pp. 226–229]
—which is
a proof of Eilenberg’s theorem in the context of monoids.

The correspondence
\mathbb{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathcal{V}
produces varieties.

First we have to show that if
\mathbb{V}
is a
pseudovariety
of synchronous algebras
and
\mathbb{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathcal{V}
, then
\mathcal{V}
is a

\ast
-pseudovarieties of synchronous relations
.
Since
\mathbb{V}
is closed under finite products,
\mathcal{V}
is closed under Boolean operations.

Syntactic derivatives
:
Then let
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
, and let
\mathcal{S}
be any other
relation

recognized
by
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
. This implies that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}}}}

divides

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
, and so
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}}}}\in\mathbb{V}
, from which
we have
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}}}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
.

Inverse morphisms
:
Lastly, if
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
, say
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}={\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}^{-1}\!}[\textrm{Acc}]
,
if
\psi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}
is a
synchronous algebra morphism
,
then
\psi^{-1}[\mathcal{R}]=({\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}\!}\circ\psi)^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
, so

\psi^{-1}[\mathcal{R}]
is
recognized
by
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
, that is

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\psi^{-1}[\mathcal{R}]}}}

divides

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}\in\mathbb{V}

and
\mathbb{V}
is closed by
division
, it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\psi^{-1}[\mathcal{R}]}}}\in\mathbb{V}

and hence
\psi^{-1}[\mathcal{R}]\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma}
.
This concludes the proof that
\mathcal{V}
is a
\ast
-pseudovariety of synchronous relations
.

Inverse bijections: part 1.
Assume that
\mathbb{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathbb{W}
.
Then

\mathcal{V}\colon\Sigma\mapsto\{\mathcal{R}\subseteq\Sigma^{*}\times\Sigma^{*}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}\in\mathbb{V}\},

and so
\mathbb{W}
is the
pseudovariety
generated by all
syntactic synchronous algebras
that
belong to
\mathbb{V}
.
It follows that
\mathbb{W}\subseteq\mathbb{V}
.
To prove that
\mathbb{V}\subseteq\mathbb{W}
, let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{V}
.
Let
\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}
be an alphabet big enough so that
there are injections from
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
to
\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}\times\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}
,
and from
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
and
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
to
\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}\times\hskip 0.50003pt\rule{3.00003pt}{0.74997pt}\hskip 0.50003pt
and
\hskip 0.50003pt\rule{3.00003pt}{0.74997pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\times\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}
,
respectively. Since
\mathbf{A}
is
locally generated
,
this allows us to define a surjective
synchronous algebra morphism

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{A}
.
We then claim that
\mathbf{A}

divides

\mathbf{B}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\prod_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}\in\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{B}_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}

where
\mathbf{B}_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}
is the
syntactic synchronous algebra

of
\varphi^{-1}[x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}]
.
Indeed, let
\psi_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{B}_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}

be the
syntactic synchronous algebra morphism
of
\varphi^{-1}[x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}]
, say

.
Then consider

\Psi\colon

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}}

\to

\mathbf{B}

u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}

\mapsto

\langle\psi_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})\rangle_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}\in\mathbf{A}}
,

and let
\mathbf{B}_{0}
be its image. Observe that for each
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}}
,

\psi_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})\in\textrm{Acc}_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}

iff

u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in\varphi^{-1}[x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}]

i.e.

\varphi(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}})=x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}
—note by the way that it implies
\sigma=\tau
.
This implies that for any
(\langle y_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}\rangle_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}\in\mathbf{A}})_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in\mathbf{B}_{0}
, there exists a unique
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}

s.t.

y_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}\in\textrm{Acc}_{x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\tau}}}
. This defines a map

\chi\colon\mathbf{B}_{0}\to\mathbf{A}
. Since moreover it makes the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma_{\mathbf{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}

it follows that
\chi
is in fact a surjective
synchronous algebra morphism
.
17
17

17

See
e.g.

[
Boj15
, Lemma 3.2, p. 10]
for a proof in a similar (but different) context.
Hence,
\mathbf{A}
is a

of
\mathbf{B}_{0}
, which is a

of

\mathbf{B}

Inverse bijections: part 2.
Assume now that
\mathcal{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathbb{V}

and
\mathbb{V}\mathop{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\to}}\mathcal{W}
. Then for each
\Sigma
, for each
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}\in\mathbb{V}
so
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Sigma}
, and hence
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
.

We then want to show the converse inclusion, namely
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. Let
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Sigma}
for some
\Sigma
,
i.e.

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}\in\mathbb{V}
.
Hence there exists
\Gamma
and
relations

\mathcal{S}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma_{1}},\ldots\mathcal{S}_{k}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma_{k}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{R}}}}

divides

\mathbf{B}\mathrel{\hat{=}}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}}\times\cdots\times{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}}
,

i.e.
there is a

\mathbf{C}\subseteq\mathbf{B}
which is a
quotient
of
\mathbf{B}
.
Then
\mathbf{C}
also
recognizes

\mathcal{R}
, say
\mathcal{R}=\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]
for some

morphism

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{C}
and
\textrm{Acc}\subseteq\mathbf{C}
.
Let
\iota\colon\mathbf{C}\to\mathbf{B}
be the canonical embedding,

\pi_{i}\colon\mathbf{B}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}}
be the canonical projection,
and
\varphi_{i}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\pi_{i}\circ\iota\circ\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}}

for
i\in\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket
. Then notice that since
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma_{i}}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}}
is surjective, then there exists
\psi_{i}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}\circ\psi_{i}=\varphi_{i}
. Indeed, it suffices
to send
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)
(resp.
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, resp.
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)
)
on any element
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma_{i}}
(resp.
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
,
resp.
u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
) such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=\varphi\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)

(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=\varphi\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
resp.
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}(u_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}})=\varphi\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\hskip 0.40974pt\rule{2.45836pt}{0.61455pt}\hskip 0.40974pt\\
a\end{smallmatrix}\right)
). Overall, the following diagram
commutes
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{A}_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}

Our goal is to show that
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
. Observe that:

{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}=\varphi^{-1}[\textrm{Acc}]=\cup_{x\in\textrm{Acc}}\varphi^{-1}[x]

but then
\textrm{Acc}\subseteq\mathbf{B}
, so
x
is a tuple
\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rangle

(all elements having the same type), and by definition:

\varphi^{-1}[x]=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\varphi^{-1}[\iota^{-1}[\pi_{i}^{-1}[x_{i}]]]=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}^{-1}[x_{i}].

But then
\varphi_{i}^{-1}[x_{i}]=\psi_{i}^{-1}[{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}^{-1}[x_{i}]]
.
Since
\mathcal{V}
is closed under
syntactic derivatives
and
\mathcal{S}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma_{i}}

we have
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}^{-1}[x_{i}]\in\mathcal{V}_{\Gamma_{i}}
, and then since
\mathcal{V}
is closed under

Inverse morphisms
and
\psi_{i}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Sigma}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathbf{S}_{2}\Gamma_{i}}
is a
morphism
between free algebras,

\psi_{i}^{-1}[{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{S}_{i}}}\!}^{-1}[x_{i}]]\in\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
.
Thus
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\underline{\mathcal{R}}}
is a
Boolean combination
of elements of
\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
, and hence
it also belongs to
\mathcal{V}_{\Sigma}
. This concludes the proof of
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
.
∎

Appendix F
Synchronous Algebras Require a Dependency Relation

In this section, we introduce the notion of
synchronous algebra
with no dependency relation,
called
naive synchronous algebra
. This notion is more natural—or naive—than

Definition

11
, and share some of its enjoyable properties, such as the existence of syntactic algebras. Yet, we show that these algebras cannot characterize some
natural classes of synchronous relation. More precisely, we show that there
is a
\ast
-pseudovariety of regular languages

\mathcal{V}
and two synchronous relations

\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, such that:

•

\mathcal{R}_{0}
is a
\mathcal{V}
-relation,

•

\mathcal{R}_{1}
is not a
\mathcal{V}
-relation,

•

\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
\mathcal{R}_{1}
have the same syntactic
naive synchronous algebra
.

Definition 35

(Naive synchronous algebra)
.

Let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.4141,0.1289,0.1211}\mathcal{T}_{1}}\!\mathrel{\hat{=}}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathcal{T}}\!\cup\{1\}
. We extend the notion of
compatibility
so
that every
\sigma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathcal{T}_{1}}\!
is
compatible
with
1
and

1
is
compatible
with
\sigma
.
A
naive synchronous algebra

\mathbf{A}
consists of a
{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\mathcal{T}_{1}}\!
-typed sets
,
together with a partial binary operator
\cdot
such that:

•

\cdot
is defined exactly on
compatible
elements and is associative, and

•

there is a unique element of type
1
, denoted by
1
, and it satisfies

x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\cdot 1=x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}=1\cdot x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}

for all
x_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\sigma}}\in\mathbf{A}
.

The set of all
synchronous words
is naturally a
naive synchronous algebra
under concatenation.
Moreover, any
synchronous relation
admits a syntactic
naive synchronous algebra
—this can be shown in the same fashion as
Lemma

18
.

Example 36

(Group relations:
Example

22
cont’d)
.

Consider the relations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\big\{(u,v)\;\big|\;

\displaystyle|u|>|v|\text{ and }(|u|-|v|\bmod{p})=0\big\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\big\{(u,v)\;\big|\;

\displaystyle|u|>|v|\text{ and }(|u|-|v|\bmod{p})=1\big\}.

Then by
Example

22
,
\mathcal{R}_{0}
is not a
group relation
but

\mathcal{R}_{1}
is.
Yet, we claim that both relations have the same syntactic
naive synchronous algebra

\mathbf{A}
, described as follows:

•

it has a unit, denoted by
0
, of type
1
,

•

\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
,
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
and
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
are all reduced to a single element,
denoted by
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
,
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
and
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
,

•

\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
and
\mathbf{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}
contain the elements
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
,

•

\cdot
is defined as the addition over
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
, by identifying

0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}1}}
,
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
,
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}
and
0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{b}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}

with the zero of
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
.

Then
\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
\mathcal{R}_{1}
are the preimages of
\{0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},0_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\}

and
\{1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{l}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}},1_{{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}{\color[rgb]{0.0859,0.2891,0.3477}\text{$\nicefrac{{\footnotesize\textsc{l}\,}}{{\,\footnotesize\textsc{b}}}$}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\}
, respectively, by the natural morphism onto
\mathbf{A}
.
And hence
\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
\mathcal{R}_{1}
are recognized by
\mathbf{A}
. It is easy to show that it is in fact the syntactic
naive synchronous algebra
of these relations: by surjectivity of the morphism
above, it suffices to show that no two elements of
\mathbf{A}
can be identified and still recognize
the same relation.

And so, from this example is follows that “being a
\mathcal{V}
-relations” cannot be characterized
by the syntactic
naive synchronous algebra
of the relation, which shows how crucial the
dependency relation
of
Definition

11
is in order to get
Section

1.3
.

The same result can be used to prove that “naive positive synchronous algebras”—defined analogously to
naive synchronous algebra
except that there is no
type
1 and no unit, and hence no empty word in the free algebra—are also unable to capture the property of “being a
\mathcal{V}
-relations”.
